> 1: # 05 — System Design Document — aios-sql-bridge
> 2: 
> 3: **BoK Lens:** SWEBOK v4 + CISSP CBK
> 4: **Sealed:** 2026-05-19
> 5: **Author:** MCORCH Master Execution Agent (Lei 1 — código fonte primário em `supabase/functions/aios-sql-bridge/`)

> 8: 
> 9: ---
> 10: 
> 11: ## 1. Purpose & Scope
> 12: 
> 13: `aios-sql-bridge` é uma Supabase Edge Function (Deno) que expõe **SELECT controlado** sobre 4 tabelas operacionais do MCORCH para a camada AIOS (skills internas, dashboards, claude-mem agents). Cumpre o papel de **Monitor primary surface** no ciclo MAPE-K:
> 14: 
> 15: - **Habilita** introspecção operacional do Sovereign sem SQL bruto, sem acesso direto ao banco, sem expor service-role key fora do edge runtime.
> 16: - **Bloqueia** acesso a colunas sensíveis (tokens, secrets, PII) por **defense-in-depth dupla**: whitelist explícito + regex global de FORBIDDEN_COLUMN_PATTERNS.
> 17: - **Audita** cada query (success + falha) em duas tabelas (`usage_tracking`, `infra_health_logs`).
> 18: - **Limita** abuso via gate de `admin` role + quota de 100 queries/dia/user.
> 19: 
> 20: **Out of scope:** INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE (read-only por design), raw SQL execution, dados de outros usuários sem admin role.
> 21:

  "CodeContent": "\"# MRD \u2014 Market Requirements Document\\n\\n**Project:** aios-sql-bridge\\n**Version:** v1.0 | **Date:** 2026-05-23\\n**BoK Applied:** BABOK v3 (Strategy Analysis + Elicitation & Collaboration)\\n**Status:** Complete\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. Executive Summary\\n\\n`aios-sql-bridge` resolves a critical internal operational constraint: how can autonomous LLM agents (operating in the OpenClaw Pantheon) and internal telemetry tools inspect production data securely and efficiently? Direct database connection by agents is highly dangerous as it risks SQL injection, exposes database secrets, and bypasses Row-Level Security (RLS). \\n\\nThis project provides a read-only, schema-whitelisted, and rate-limited gateway that serves as the primary **Monitor** interface within the MAPE-K autonomic loop.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Market Problem Statement\\n\\n### 2.1 Current State\\n\\nPreviously, agents and administrative pages lacked a unified, secure, and low-latency mechanism to query historical run statistics, transaction balances, and infrastructure status. \\nRunning raw SQL queries client-side or within agent prompts leads to:\\n1. **Severe Security Risks**: Exposing the service-role key or requiring direct postgres credentials.\\n2. **Data Exfiltration**: Agents could scan sensitive user details (emails, stripe tokens, access tokens).\\n3. **Data Pollution & Abuse**: No rate limiting or query quota bounds, risking database denial of service.\\n\\n### 2.2 Root Cause Analysis\\n\\nThe lack of a secure virtualization layer. In an agentic ecosystem, agents need database introspection to adjust their behaviors (autonomic self-healing), but they cannot be trusted with raw DB connection strings. A restricted SQL-like query interface is required to bridge the gap.\\n\\n### 2.3 Desired State\\n\\nA secure Supabase Edge Function that exposes a strict **Filter Objects contract** (no raw SQL inputs). It parses parameters, validates table whitelists, drops sensitive columns, enforces an admin-role authorization, tracks API usage in logs, and restricts daily quotas.\\n\\n---\\n\\\n<truncated 3770 bytes>",

Currently, the engineering team and autonomous agents need access to database metrics (such as coin spending, run histories, profile balances). \\n\\n`aios-sql-bridge` provides the business-level assurance that this data can be retrieved with zero risk of database mutation, zero exposure of secret keys, and strict volume limits.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Business Goals (SMART Objectives)\\n\\n* **BO-001 (Security)**: Zero leakages of Stripe credentials, access tokens, or personal identifiers (PII) through the bridge query interface.\\n* **BO-002 (Performance)**: Maintain a query execution P95 latency below 200ms.\\n* **BO-003 (Governance)**: 100% of queries executed must be logged in `usage_tracking` for auditing.\\n* **BO-004 (Control)**: A maximum quota of 100 requests per day per user is enforced to prevent resource abuse.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. Business Rules\\n\\n### BR-001: Read-Only Enforcement\\nThe service is strictly read-only.   "CodeContent": "\"# PRD \u2014 Product Requirements Document\\n\\n**Project:** aios-sql-bridge\\n**Version:** v1.0 | **Date:** 2026-05-23\\n**BoK Applied:** ProdBOK + CXBOK (Product Management + User Experience Design)\\n**Status:** Complete\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. Product Vision\\n\\nProvide a highly secure, developer-friendly, and predictable data introspection endpoint for internal tools (Dashboards, HUDs, and Agents) that allows read-only query capabilities over database statistics while guaranteeing 100% compliance with data privacy (LGPD) and system integrity.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. User Personas\\n\\n### Persona 1: Gabriel (Sovereign)\\n* **Needs**: Run diagnostic queries to detect ledger drifts or campaign states without opening psql consoles or risking data integrity.\\n* **Pain points**: Manual database lookup is slow; raw SQL exposes sensitive tokens and credit card keys.\\n\\n### Persona 2: Master Execution Agent (AI)\\n* **Needs**: Introspect historical campaign run metrics and client transaction logs to execute self-healing loops and adjust costs.\\n* **Pain points**: Hardcoded REST endpoints lack filtering power; direct SQL access is a major security risk.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. Gherkin Acceptance Criteria\\n\\n### Scenario 1: Query profiles as Admin\\n```gherkin\\nGiven a user is authenticated with a valid Supabase token\\nAnd the user has the \\\"admin\\\" role\\nWhen they request \\\"profiles\\\" table with a limit of 1\\nThen the system returns HTTP 200 OK\\nAnd the payload contains a list of profiles, row count, execution time, and query hash\\n```\\n\\n### Scenario 2: Standard user is blocked\\n```gherkin\\nGiven a user is authenticated with a valid Supabase token\\nAnd the user has the \\\"viewer\\\" role\\nWhen they query the bridge\\nThen the system returns HTTP 403 Forbidden\\nAnd the message states \\\"Admin role required\\\"\\n```\\n\\n### Scenario 3: Projection blocklist triggered\\n```gherkin\\nGiven an Admin user requests \\\"mcoin_transactions\\\" table\\nAnd they request the \\\"access_token\\\" or similar sensitive column\\nWhen the request is parsed\\nThen the system returns HTTP 400\n<truncated 742 bytes>",

Functional Requirements\\n\\n### FR-001: Auth & JWT Processing\\n* **Description**: The endpoint must extract the bearer token from the HTTP `Authorization` header and query Supabase Auth (`admin.auth.getUser(token)`) to resolve the calling user's UUID.\\n* **Failure response**: Return HTTP 401 Unauthorized if missing, malformed, or expired.\\n\\n### FR-002: Admin Role Verification\\n* **Description**: Execute a database RPC check `has_role(user_id, 'admin')` using the service-role client.\\n* **Failure response**: Return HTTP 403 Forbidden with a clear permission error if the user is not an administrator.\\n\\n### FR-003: Daily Quota Enforcement\\n* **Description**: Query `usage_tracking` for rows where `user_id = calling_user_id`, `resource_type = 'sql_bridge_query'`, and `created_at` is within the current UTC calendar day.\\n* **Limit**: If count >= 100, return HTTP 429 Too Many Requests.\\n\\n### FR-004: Table & Column Whitelisting\\n* **Description**: Verify the target `table` is exactly one of `mcoin_transactions`, `pipeline_runs`, `profiles`, or `vm_canvas_executions`. Verify all projected columns inside the `select` statement exist in the table's whitelist sch\n<truncated 2022 bytes>",

  "CodeContent": "\"# Data & Knowledge Model\\n\\n**Project:** aios-sql-bridge\\n**Version:** v1.0 | **Date:** 2026-05-23\\n**BoK Applied:** DMBOK v2 (Data Integration & Interoperability + Data Security)\\n**Status:** Complete\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. Whitelisted Logical Schema Mappings\\n\\nThe bridge exposes select columns from four operational tables:\\n\\n### 1.1 `profiles`\\n* **Purpose**: User balance and score tracking.\\n* **Whitelisted Columns**: `id`, `mco_balance`, `score`, `created_at`, `updated_at`.\\n* **Default Projection**: `id, mco_balance, score, created_at, updated_at`.\\n\\n### 1.2 `mcoin_transactions`\\n* **Purpose**: Token ledgers.\\n* **Whitelisted Columns**: `id`, `user_id`, `action`, `amount`, `created_at`, `context`, `project_id`.\\n* **Default Projection**: `id, user_id, action, amount, created_at, context, project_id`.\\n\\n### 1.3 `pipeline_runs`\\n* **Purpose**: Campaign execution runs.\\n* **Whitelisted Columns**: `id`, `user_id`, `topic`, `status`, `mco_cost`, `started_at`, `completed_at`.\\n* **Default Projection**: `id, user_id, topic, status, mco_cost, started_at, completed_at`.\\n\\n### 1.4 `vm_canvas_executions`\\n* **Purpose**: Canvas video/image generations.\\n* **Whitelisted Columns**: `id`, `user_id`, `provider`, `model`, `status`, `credits_charged`, `operation_id`, `webhook_received_at`, `created_at`.\\n* **Default Projection**: `id, user_id, provider, model, status, credits_charged, operation_id, webhook_received_at, created_at`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Forbidden Columns Specification (Safety Projection Rules)\\n\\nA rigid regex matches column names to prevent data exfiltration. If any string in the `select` projection matches the regex below, it is rejected immediately:\\n\\n```regexp\\n/access_token|refresh_token|stripe_|secret|password|email|phone|api_key/i\\n```\\n\\n### Affected Database Fields Protected:\\n* `user_api_keys.groq_api_key`, `user_api_keys.openrouter_api_key`, etc.\\n* `social_credentials.access_token`, `social_credentials.refresh_token`.\\n* Stripe client IDs and customer subscription tokens.\\n* Personal identifiable inform\n<truncated 1307 bytes>",

  "CodeContent": "\"# Self-Optimization Specification\\n\\n**Project:** aios-sql-bridge\\n**Version:** v1.0 | **Date:** 2026-05-23\\n**BoK Applied:** CMQ/OE BOK (Quality Management + Operational Excellence)\\n**Status:** Complete\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. Quality Objectives\\n\\n* Ensure 100% of API database calls are verified and audited.\\n* Prevent query injection attacks.\\n* Keep service execution latency below 100ms on hot-paths.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Key Performance Indicators (KPIs)\\n\\n| KPI ID | Metric Name | Target | Measurement Source |\\n|--------|-------------|--------|--------------------|\\n| KPI-QL-001 | Bridge Availability | 99.9% | Supabase edge function logs |\\n| KPI-QL-002 | Execution Latency (P95) | < 200ms | `usage_tracking.durationMs` |\\n| KPI-QL-003 | Failed Telemetry Audits | 0% | `infra_health_logs` where `status = 'unhealthy'` |\\n| KPI-QL-004 | Security Violations Caught | 100% | Rejected column projection metrics |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. Failure Mode and Effects Analysis (FMEA)\\n\\n| ID | Failure Mode | Severity (S) | Occurrence (O) | Detection (D) | RPN | Mitigation |\\n|----|--------------|--------------|----------------|---------------|-----|------------|\\n| FMEA-001 | SQL Injection bypass through parameters | 9 | 1 | 2 | **18** | Parameterized inputs in supabase-js SDK, strict whitelists |\\n| FMEA-002 | Secret token exposure | 10 | 1 | 2 | **20** | Double verification: table whitelist + global forbidden regex |\\n| FMEA-003 | Quota count race condition (TOCTOU) | 4 | 3 | 5 | **60** | Acceptable risk (maximum exfiltration bounds are low) |\\n| FMEA-004 | Logging failure silences violations | 7 | 2 | 4 | **56** | Log to both postgres table and edge standard output |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. Self-Healing & Telemetry\\n\\n* **Watchdog pulse**: The function emits a status pulse (`healthy` / `unhealthy`) to `infra_health_logs` on each run.\\n* **Degraded mode**: If the audit database is down, the function falls back to standard logs (`console.error`), continuing to serve queries to prevent system-wide dashboard blockages.\\n\"",

> 1: # SOP — Orchestration content_mesh_asset Emission
> 2: 
> 3: **Versão:** v1 · **Selada:** 2026-05-19 · **Lei 2 (Processo Antecipado)** · **OE-661 Phase 1**
> 4: 
> 5: ## ORO triplet
> 6: 
> 7: - **Operator:** `orchestrate-content` Edge Function (automatizado por trigger de campanha do Usuário Zero ou de cliente)
> 8: - **Reviewer:** Sovereign (Gabriel) — aprova primeira execução e valida campos da row no Supabase Studio
> 9: - **Owner:** Sovereign — risco reputacional do post WordPress e risco financeiro do affiliate token (`GCRUX_ML_AFFILIATE_TOKEN`)
> 10: 
> 11: ## Contexto
> 12: 
> 13: A função `orchestrate-content` já materializa o flywheel completo (article → WordPress → social → observation node). Falta a peça que conecta o output a Link Forge: um nó `mcorch_nodes(node_type='content_mesh_asset')` carregando o `articleContent` no campo `content`, com `metadata.wordpress_url` populado e `project_id='mcorch-affiliate'`. Link Forge (`scripts/link-forge.ts`) varre essa tabela buscando matches de regex de produtos ML; sem a emissão, o flywheel para no observation node e nunca vira receita monetizável.
> 14: 
> 15: **Por que existe:** A v6.6.3 fechou todas as dependências (postback → ATTRIBUTES_REVENUE_TO; auto-stitch observation → file; Link Forge dry-run validado). O único nó faltante é o asset emit. Sem este SOP + código, **o flywheel afiliado nunca rodou end-to-end uma vez em produção** (3 dias com `revenue_cents=0`).
> 16: 
> 17: **Decisão de design:** emissão é **paralela** à observation (não sequencial) via `Prom
> <truncated 6710 bytes>
> ido
> 100: - `bun run scripts/link-forge.ts --dry-run` retorna matches > 0 em pelo menos 1 dos novos assets
> 101: - Mesh `infra_health_logs` registra `service='orchestrate-content' status='healthy'` (pulse já existente)
> 102: 
> 103: ## Anti-patterns
> 104: 
> 105: - ❌ **Try/catch separado para observation + asset** — quebra Amendment A (Bug A): cobrança 10 mcoCoins com mesh half-emitted, sem rastreabilidade granular do que falhou.
> 106: - ❌ **Emit asset sem checar `wpPostUrl`** — Bug B do audit; Link Forge consome row órfã, tenta republish em URL inexistente.
> 107: - ❌ **`project_id` ausente no shape** — quebra Amendment C: Link Forge sem filter de tenant scan = risco de cross-tenant leak quando primeiro cliente externo entrar.
> 108: - ❌ **`content` truncado ou só metadados** — Link Forge precisa do body completo para regex scan; truncar = matches perdidos = revenue perdido.
> 109: - ❌ **Sync block waiting embedding** — embedding é assíncrono via trigger pg_net; aguardar quebra latência de pipeline e não traz valor (Link Forge não depende de embedding).
> 110: - ❌ **Skipped marked as error** — confunde análise de saúde; `skipped` é decisão deliberada (Amendment B gate), `error` é bug real.
> 111:

<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Analyzing Metadata Now**

I've just accessed the metadata for `f3a091f7-9e72-4927-b0cb-2b65cffa9844`. It contains a `link_id` and a `content_id`. I'm now looking into the `handle-ml-postback/index.ts` script to see what it's doing with this information. I am currently reviewing the use of `orFilter`.


**Clarifying Query Logic**

I've determined the `orFilter` targets `mcorch_nodes`, selecting a `content_mesh_asset` based on either `link_id` or `content_id` within the `metadata` column. I have validated that the `content_id` in the metadata, is actually the node `id` itself. I now recognize that this could introduce a potential inefficiency, since it searches indirectly.


**Refining Node Identification**

I'm now zeroing in on why the query logic in `handle-ml-postback/index.ts` uses `metadata->>content_id` instead of directly querying the `id` field of the node. It appears legacy nodes had this metadata, but new `content_mesh_asset` nodes, created via `orchestrate-content/index.ts`, don't. However, `process-affiliate-link` populates `affiliate_links` with the node's `content_id`. The postback logic seems inefficient, and could be simplified to use the node ID directly.


**Identifying Critical Bug**

I've discovered a critical bug in `handle-ml-postback/index.ts`. The query logic incorrectly uses `metadata->>content_id` for `content_mesh_asset` nodes, when it should use `id`. This means it searches the wrong field when looking up a node. The correct filter should include a direct `id` check. I'm now certain this explains a recent test failure.

</details>

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Analyzing the Constraint Violation**

Okay, so I've hit a snag. It appears a unique constraint on the `mcorch_nodes` table, specifically on the `name` column, is being violated. The error message is clear: a key value already exists. The name in question is "conversion:9b8f9a7d". Digging deeper, it is clear that this key must be unique, and that I may not be able to proceed without resolving this constraint!


**Clarifying the Root Cause**

I've got it! The `name` uniqueness on `mcorch_nodes` means only one conversion node per affiliate link is allowed. The first run successfully created "conversion:9b8f9a7d," but because the `node` was missing, it didn't create the edge. The second run failed because the node already existed! Now I can move forward on implementing a fix!

</details>

> 225:             </Button>

> 1: # SOP — Handoff Material Proof Audit v1.0
> 2: 
> 3: **Versão:** v1 · **Selada:** 2026-05-19 · **Lei 2 (Processo Antecipado)** · Trigger: `/handoff` Fase 5c, OU auditoria on-demand de um selo via `bun run scripts/qa/run-audit.ts`
> 4: 
> 5: > Esta SOP documenta o **processo humano** de verificação de prova material de um selo `/handoff`.
> 6: > O skill `mcorch-qa-healing` (`scripts/qa/run-audit.ts`) **automatiza exatamente esta sequência**.
> 7: > Se um humano não consegue executá-la sem erro, o script também não conseguirá — Lei 2.
> 8: 
> 9: ## ORO triplet
> 10: 
> 11: - **Operator:** o agente que executa `/handoff` (MCORCH Master Execution Agent). Fallback manual = o Sovereign rodando a Sequence à mão no shell. Após o v1 do `mcorch-qa-healing`, o Operator usa o script; esta SOP é a referência manual de quando o script não está disponível ou precisa ser auditado.
> 12: - **Reviewer:** Sovereign (Gabriel) — revê o Proof Manifest no relatório da Fase 8.
> 13: - **Owner:** Sovereign até v6.4.x; migra para o `engineer` agent depois. Risco material: um selo com claim de SUCCESS falsa (commit inexistente, UUID que não resolve, teste que não passou) chega ao `origin/main` e corrompe o ledger de handoff — exatamente a morte por dívida invisível que a SSP-01 Lei 1 existe para prevenir.
> 14: 
> 15: ## Contexto
> 16: 
> 17: O ritual `/handoff` (`.claude/commands/handoff.md`) sela cada sessão em 8 fases e encerra com o bloco **Survival Laws Self-audit** (`.claude/rules/survival.md`, "Self-audit cadence"). O item de Lei 1 — *"Toda claim de SUCCESS
> <truncated 5420 bytes>
> do com **toda** claim em `pass` ou `skip` explícito — zero `fail`.
> 64: - Linha de veredito = `QA VERDICT: <N> verified · <M> skipped · 0 failed → SEAL ALLOWED`.
> 65: - Uma linha em `infra_health_logs` com `service='qa-healing'`, `status='healthy'` (ou `degraded` se houve `skip`).
> 66: - A Fase 5c do `/handoff` libera a passagem para a Fase 6; o selo segue para push.

> 68: ## Anti-patterns
> 69: 
> 70: - ❌ Marcar uma claim como `pass` sem rodar o comando de verificação correspondente — é auto-avaliação, exatamente o vício que a Fase 5c existe para matar.
> 71: - ❌ Fabricar um UUID, hash ou contagem de teste para fazer uma claim contradita "passar" — violação direta de Lei 1; halt imediato.
> 72: - ❌ Tratar erro de rede / ferramenta ausente como `fail` — bloqueio falso; o correto é `skip` + pulse `degraded`.
> 73: - ❌ Rodar `/handoff` pulando a Fase 5c — o gate é silenciosamente burlado (FMEA-007); a Fase 5c está na lista de Invariants do `handoff.md` justamente por isso.
> 74: - ❌ Auto-corrigir o texto do `HANDOFF.md` para um claim bater — verificação NÃO é edição; a única cura permitida no v1 é o re-INSERT do nó de handoff (Cenário D).
> 75:

> 1: # MRD — Market Requirements Document
> 2: 
> 3: **Project:** aios-sql-bridge
> 4: **Version:** v1.0 | **Date:** 2026-05-23
> 5: **BoK Applied:** BABOK v3 (Strategy Analysis + Elicitation & Collaboration)
> 6: **Status:** Complete
> 7: 
> 8: ---
> 9: 
> 10: ## 1. Executive Summary
> 11: 
> 12: `aios-sql-bridge` resolves a critical internal operational constraint: how can autonomous LLM agents (operating in the OpenClaw Pantheon) and internal telemetry tools inspect production data securely and efficiently? Direct database connection by agents is highly dangerous as it risks SQL injection, exposes database secrets, and bypasses Row-Level Security (RLS). 
> 13: 
> 14: This project provides a read-only, schema-whitelisted, and rate-limited gateway that serves as the primary **Monitor** interface within the MAPE-K autonomic loop.
> 15: 
> 16: ---
> 17: 
> 18: ## 2. Market Problem Statement
> 19: 
> 20: ### 2.1 Current State
> 21: 
> 22: Previously, agents and administrative pages lacked a unified, secure, and low-latency mechanism to query historical run statistics, transaction balances, and infrastructure status. 
> 23: Running raw SQL queries client-side or within agent prompts leads to:
> 24: 1. **Severe Security Risks**: Exposing the service-role key or requiring direct postgres credentials.
> 25: 2. **Data Exfiltration**: Agents could scan sensitive user details (emails, stripe tokens, access tokens).
> 26: 3. **Data Pollution & Abuse**: No rate limiting or query quota bounds, risking database denial of service.
> 27: 
> 28: ### 2.2 Root Cause Analysis
> 29: 
> 30: The lack of a secure virtualization layer. 
> <truncated 2580 bytes>
> ity | Rationale | Source |
> 69: |----|-------------------|----------|-----------|--------|
> 70: | MR-001 | Query capability must be strictly read-only | Critical | Prevent malicious state modification by agents | SSP-01 Law 1 |
> 71: | MR-002 | Access must be gated to verified Admin roles only | Critical | Prevent privilege escalation by standard users | Security design |
> 72: | MR-003 | Input format must be structured (no raw SQL strings allowed) | Critical | Completely eliminate standard SQL injection vectors | SWEBOK v4 |
> 73: | MR-004 | Column filter projection must enforce blocklist rules dynamically | Critical | Ensure compliance with LGPD by hiding PII/Secrets | LGPD Compliance |
> 74: | MR-005 | Daily request limits must be applied per user | High | Prevent denial of service or abuse loops | SWEBOK v4 |
> 75: 
> 76: ---
> 77:

> 81: |-----|-----------|--------|-------------|

> 83: | Security Incidents | Unauthorized access attempts successful | 0 | `infra_health_logs` alerts |
> 84: | Whitelist Drift | Failures due to missing schema definitions | < 1% | `usage_tracking` error logs |
